```
1→"""Structured evidence parsing and validation for triage stage submissions."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     7→
     8→
     9→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    10→# Shared constants
    11→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12→
    13→VERDICT_KEYWORDS = frozenset({
    14→    "genuine",
    15→    "false positive",
    16→    "false-positive",
    17→    "exaggerated",
    18→    "over-engineering",
    19→    "over engineering",
    20→})
    21→
    22→# Permissive path regex — detects whether *any* file path was mentioned.
    23→# NOT used to validate existence (that's enrich-only in validation.enrich_checks).
    24→_EVIDENCE_PATH_RE = re.compile(r"[\w.@-]+/[\w./@-]+\.\w+")
    25→
    26→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    27→# Data classes
    28→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    29→
    30→@dataclass
    31→class EvidenceFailure:
    32→    """A single evidence validation failure."""
    33→
    34→    code: str
    35→    message: str
    36→    blocking: bool = True
    37→
    38→
    39→@dataclass
    40→class ObserveAssessment:
    41→    """A parsed per-issue assessment from an observe report (template format)."""
    42→
    43→    issue_hash: str
    44→    verdict: str  # normalised keyword
    45→    verdict_reasoning: str = ""
    46→    files_read: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    47→    recommendation: str = ""
    48→
    49→
    50→@dataclass
    51→class ObserveEvidence:
    52→    """Parsed observe-report evidence."""
    53→
    54→    entries: list[ObserveAssessment] = field(default_factory=list)
    55→    unparsed_citation_count: int = 0  # hashes cited without a verdict
    56→    has_parseable_ids: bool = True  # False if valid_ids had no hex-hash IDs
    57→
    58→
    59→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    60→# OBSERVE evidence parsing — structured template format
    61→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    62→
    63→def _normalise_verdict(raw: str) -> str | None:
    64→    """Return a normalised verdict keyword, or None if not recognised."""
    65→    lower = raw.lower().strip()
    66→    for kw in VERDICT_KEYWORDS:
    67→        if kw in lower:
    68→            # Normalise to canonical form
    69→            canonical = kw.replace("-", " ")
    70→            return canonical
    71→    return None
    72→
    73→
    74→def _build_short_map(valid_ids: set[str]) -> dict[str, str]:
    75→    """Build short-hash → full-id map from valid issue IDs."""
    76→    short_map: dict[str, str] = {}
    77→    for fid in valid_ids:
    78→        short = fid.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
    79→        if re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{6,}", short):
    80→            short_map[short] = fid
    81→    return short_map
    82→
    83→
    84→def _is_valid_hash(raw_hash: str, short_map: dict[str, str], valid_ids: set[str]) -> bool:
    85→    """Check if a hash is known in either the short map or valid IDs."""
    86→    return raw_hash in short_map or raw_hash in valid_ids
    87→
    88→
    89→# --- YAML-like template parser ---
    90→
    91→# Matches lines like:  - hash: abc12345  or  hash: abc12345
    92→_YAML_HASH_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*-?\s*hash\s*:\s*([0-9a-f]{6,})", re.IGNORECASE)
    93→# Matches lines like:  verdict: genuine  or  verdict: false-positive
    94→_YAML_VERDICT_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*verdict\s*:\s*(.+)", re.IGNORECASE)
    95→# Matches lines like:  verdict_reasoning: some reason
    96→_YAML_REASONING_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*verdict_reasoning\s*:\s*(.+)", re.IGNORECASE)
    97→# Matches lines like:  files_read: [src/foo.ts, src/bar.ts]  or  files_read: src/foo.ts
    98→_YAML_FILES_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*files_read\s*:\s*(.+)", re.IGNORECASE)
    99→# Matches lines like:  recommendation: do something
   100→_YAML_RECOMMENDATION_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*recommendation\s*:\s*(.+)", re.IGNORECASE)
   101→
   102→def _parse_yaml_files_field(raw: str) -> list[str]:
   103→    """Parse the files_read field — supports [a, b] list or single path."""
   104→    raw = raw.strip()
   105→    # Strip surrounding brackets
   106→    if raw.startswith("[") and raw.endswith("]"):
   107→        raw = raw[1:-1]
   108→    # Split on commas
   109→    paths = [p.strip().strip("'\"") for p in raw.split(",")]
   110→    return [p for p in paths if p]
   111→
   112→
   113→def _flush_yaml_entry(
   114→    current: dict,
   115→    short_map: dict[str, str],
   116→    valid_ids: set[str],
   117→) -> ObserveAssessment | None:
   118→    """Convert a collected YAML-like entry dict into an ObserveAssessment, or None if invalid."""
   119→    raw_hash = current.get("hash", "").lower()
   120→    if not raw_hash or not _is_valid_hash(raw_hash, short_map, valid_ids):
   121→        return None
   122→    raw_verdict = current.get("verdict", "")
   123→    verdict = _normalise_verdict(raw_verdict)
   124→    if not verdict:
   125→        return None
   126→    return ObserveAssessment(
   127→        issue_hash=raw_hash,
   128→        verdict=verdict,
   129→        verdict_reasoning=current.get("verdict_reasoning", ""),
   130→        files_read=current.get("files_read", []),
   131→        recommendation=current.get("recommendation", ""),
   132→    )
   133→
   134→
   135→def _parse_yaml_template(
   136→    report: str,
   137→    short_map: dict[str, str],
   138→    valid_ids: set[str],
   139→) -> list[ObserveAssessment]:
   140→    """Parse YAML-like template entries from report text."""
   141→    entries: list[ObserveAssessment] = []
   142→    current: dict | None = None
   143→
   144→    for line in report.splitlines():
   145→        m_hash = _YAML_HASH_RE.match(line)
   146→        if m_hash:
   147→            # Flush previous entry
   148→            if current is not None:
   149→                entry = _flush_yaml_entry(current, short_map, valid_ids)
   150→                if entry:
   151→                    entries.append(entry)
   152→            current = {"hash": m_hash.group(1).lower()}
   153→            continue
   154→
   155→        if current is None:
   156→            continue
   157→
   158→        m_verdict = _YAML_VERDICT_RE.match(line)
   159→        if m_verdict:
   160→            current["verdict"] = m_verdict.group(1).strip()
   161→            continue
   162→
   163→        m_reasoning = _YAML_REASONING_RE.match(line)
   164→        if m_reasoning:
   165→            current["verdict_reasoning"] = m_reasoning.group(1).strip()
   166→            continue
   167→
   168→        m_files = _YAML_FILES_RE.match(line)
   169→        if m_files:
   170→            current["files_read"] = _parse_yaml_files_field(m_files.group(1))
   171→            continue
   172→
   173→        m_rec = _YAML_RECOMMENDATION_RE.match(line)
   174→        if m_rec:
   175→            current["recommendation"] = m_rec.group(1).strip()
   176→            continue
   177→
   178→    # Flush final entry
   179→    if current is not None:
   180→        entry = _flush_yaml_entry(current, short_map, valid_ids)
   181→        if entry:
   182→            entries.append(entry)
   183→
   184→    return entries
   185→
   186→
   187→def parse_observe_evidence(report: str, valid_ids: set[str]) -> ObserveEvidence:
   188→    """Parse assessment entries from an observe-stage report.
   189→
   190→    Supports the YAML-like template format:
   191→    hash/verdict/verdict_reasoning/files_read/recommendation
   192→    """
   193→    short_map = _build_short_map(valid_ids)
   194→    entries = _parse_yaml_template(report, short_map, valid_ids)
   195→
   196→    # Count hashes that appear in report but weren't parsed as entries
   197→    all_hashes_in_report = set(re.findall(r"[0-9a-f]{8,}", report.lower()))
   198→    valid_short_hashes = set(short_map.keys())
   199→    cited_hashes = all_hashes_in_report & valid_short_hashes
   200→    parsed_hashes = {e.issue_hash for e in entries}
   201→    unparsed = len(cited_hashes - parsed_hashes)
   202→
   203→    return ObserveEvidence(
   204→        entries=entries,
   205→        unparsed_citation_count=unparsed,
   206→        has_parseable_ids=bool(short_map),
   207→    )
   208→
   209→
   210→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   211→# OBSERVE evidence validation — field presence only
   212→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   213→
   214→_OBSERVE_TEMPLATE_HINT = (
   215→    "  Required template per issue:\n"
   216→    "    - hash: <hash>\n"
   217→    "      verdict: genuine | false-positive | exaggerated | over-engineering\n"
   218→    "      verdict_reasoning: <why this verdict>\n"
   219→    "      files_read: [<file paths>]\n"
   220→    "      recommendation: <what to do>"
   221→)
   222→
   223→
   224→def validate_observe_evidence(
   225→    evidence: ObserveEvidence,
   226→    issue_count: int,
   227→) -> list[EvidenceFailure]:
   228→    """Validate parsed observe evidence — field presence only, no quality thresholds."""
   229→    if issue_count == 0:
   230→        return []
   231→
   232→    # If valid_ids contained no hex-hash IDs, verdict parsing is impossible — skip.
   233→    if not evidence.has_parseable_ids:
   234→        return []
   235→
   236→    failures: list[EvidenceFailure] = []
   237→
   238→    if not evidence.entries:
   239→        failures.append(EvidenceFailure(
   240→            code="no_verdicts",
   241→            message=(
   242→                "No per-issue assessment entries found in report.\n"
   243→                "\n"
   244→                f"{_OBSERVE_TEMPLATE_HINT}\n"
   245→                "\n"
   246→                "  Example:\n"
   247→                "    - hash: 34580232\n"
   248→                "      verdict: false-positive\n"
   249→                "      verdict_reasoning: Uses branded string union KnownTaskType\n"
   250→                "      files_read: [src/types/database.ts]\n"
   251→                "      recommendation: No action needed"
   252→            ),
   253→        ))
   254→        return failures
   255→
   256→    # Check field presence — no quality thresholds
   257→    missing_reasoning: list[ObserveAssessment] = []
   258→    missing_files: list[ObserveAssessment] = []
   259→    missing_recommendation: list[ObserveAssessment] = []
   260→
   261→    for entry in evidence.entries:
   262→        if not entry.verdict_reasoning.strip():
   263→            missing_reasoning.append(entry)
   264→        if not entry.files_read:
   265→            missing_files.append(entry)
   266→        if not entry.recommendation.strip():
   267→            missing_recommendation.append(entry)
   268→
   269→    if missing_reasoning:
   270→        examples = "\n".join(
   271→            f"    [{e.issue_hash}] {e.verdict}" for e in missing_reasoning[:5]
   272→        )
   273→        failures.append(EvidenceFailure(
   274→            code="missing_verdict_reasoning",
   275→            message=(
   276→                f"{len(missing_reasoning)} of {len(evidence.entries)} assessment(s) "
   277→                f"have no verdict_reasoning.\n"
   278→                "\n"
   279→                f"{_OBSERVE_TEMPLATE_HINT}\n"
   280→                "\n"
   281→                f"  Entries missing reasoning:\n{examples}"
   282→            ),
   283→        ))
   284→
   285→    if missing_files:
   286→        examples = "\n".join(
   287→            f"    [{e.issue_hash}] {e.verdict}" for e in missing_files[:5]
   288→        )
   289→        failures.append(EvidenceFailure(
   290→            code="missing_files_read",
   291→            message=(
   292→                f"{len(missing_files)} of {len(evidence.entries)} assessment(s) "
   293→                f"have no files_read.\n"
   294→                "\n"
   295→                f"{_OBSERVE_TEMPLATE_HINT}\n"
   296→                "\n"
   297→                f"  Entries missing files_read:\n{examples}"
   298→            ),
   299→        ))
   300→
   301→    if missing_recommendation:
   302→        examples = "\n".join(
   303→            f"    [{e.issue_hash}] {e.verdict}" for e in missing_recommendation[:5]
   304→        )
   305→        failures.append(EvidenceFailure(
   306→            code="missing_recommendation",
   307→            message=(
   308→                f"{len(missing_recommendation)} of {len(evidence.entries)} assessment(s) "
   309→                f"have no recommendation.\n"
   310→                "\n"
   311→                f"{_OBSERVE_TEMPLATE_HINT}\n"
   312→                "\n"
   313→                f"  Entries missing recommendation:\n{examples}"
   314→            ),
   315→        ))
   316→
   317→    return failures
   318→
   319→
   320→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   321→# REFLECT skip-reason validation
   322→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   323→
   324→_SKIP_LINE_RE = re.compile(
   325→    r"(?:skip|dismiss|defer|drop|remove)\s*:\s*\[?([0-9a-f]{6,})\]?\s*[(\-—–:]*\s*(.+)",
   326→    re.IGNORECASE,
   327→)
   328→
   329→
   330→def validate_reflect_skip_evidence(report: str) -> list[EvidenceFailure]:
   331→    """Validate that skip reasons in a reflect report are non-empty."""
   332→    failures: list[EvidenceFailure] = []
   333→    bad_skips: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
   334→
   335→    for line in report.splitlines():
   336→        m = _SKIP_LINE_RE.search(line)
   337→        if not m:
   338→            continue
   339→        issue_hash = m.group(1)
   340→        reason = m.group(2).strip().rstrip(")")
   341→
   342→        # Just check non-empty reason
   343→        if not reason.strip():
   344→            bad_skips.append((issue_hash, reason))
   345→
   346→    if bad_skips:
   347→        failing_examples = "\n".join(
   348→            f'    [{h}] "{r}"' for h, r in bad_skips[:5]
   349→        )
   350→        failures.append(EvidenceFailure(
   351→            code="vague_skip_reason",
   352→            message=(
   353→                f"{len(bad_skips)} skip reason(s) are empty.\n"
   354→                "\n"
   355→                "  Required format:\n"
   356→                "    Skip: [<hash>] (<reason for skipping>)\n"
   357→                "\n"
   358→                f"  Failing skips:\n{failing_examples}"
   359→            ),
   360→        ))
   361→
   362→    return failures
   363→
   364→
   365→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   366→# Cluster-name mention check (organize + sense-check)
   367→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   368→
   369→def validate_report_references_clusters(
   370→    report: str,
   371→    cluster_names: list[str],
   372→) -> list[EvidenceFailure]:
   373→    """Check that at least one cluster name appears in the report."""
   374→    if not cluster_names:
   375→        return []
   376→
   377→    report_lower = report.lower()
   378→    for name in cluster_names:
   379→        if name.lower() in report_lower:
   380→            return []
   381→
   382→    failures: list[EvidenceFailure] = []
   383→    names_str = ", ".join(cluster_names[:5])
   384→    if len(cluster_names) > 5:
   385→        names_str += f" ... ({len(cluster_names)} total)"
   386→    failures.append(EvidenceFailure(
   387→        code="no_cluster_mention",
   388→        message=(
   389→            f"Report references none of the {len(cluster_names)} cluster name(s).\n"
   390→            "\n"
   391→            "  The report must mention at least one cluster by name to prove\n"
   392→            "  awareness of the plan structure.\n"
   393→            "\n"
   394→            f"  Cluster names: {names_str}"
   395→        ),
   396→    ))
   397→    return failures
   398→
   399→
   400→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   401→# File path mention check (sense-check)
   402→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   403→
   404→def validate_report_has_file_paths(report: str) -> list[EvidenceFailure]:
   405→    """Check that at least one file path appears in the report."""
   406→    if _EVIDENCE_PATH_RE.search(report):
   407→        return []
   408→    return [EvidenceFailure(
   409→        code="no_file_paths_in_report",
   410→        message=(
   411→            "Report references no file paths (need at least 1).\n"
   412→            "\n"
   413→            "  The sense-check report must prove code was read. Include specific\n"
   414→            "  file paths and line numbers for verified steps.\n"
   415→            "\n"
   416→            "  Example:\n"
   417→            "    Verified src/services/funds.ts lines 45-67: function signature\n"
   418→            "    matches step description. Effort tag 'small' is accurate."
   419→        ),
   420→    )]
   421→
   422→
   423→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   424→# Shared output helpers
   425→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   426→
   427→def format_evidence_failures(
   428→    failures: list[EvidenceFailure],
   429→    *,
   430→    stage_label: str,
   431→) -> str:
   432→    """Format evidence failures into a single rejection message."""
   433→    blocking = [f for f in failures if f.blocking]
   434→    advisory = [f for f in failures if not f.blocking]
   435→    parts: list[str] = []
   436→
   437→    for f in blocking:
   438→        parts.append(f"  REJECTED: {f.message}")
   439→
   440→    for f in advisory:
   441→        parts.append(f"  Warning: {f.message}")
   442→
   443→    return "\n\n".join(parts)
   444→
   445→
   446→__all__ = [
   447→    "EvidenceFailure",
   448→    "ObserveAssessment",
   449→    "ObserveEvidence",
   450→    "VERDICT_KEYWORDS",
   451→    "format_evidence_failures",
   452→    "parse_observe_evidence",
   453→    "validate_observe_evidence",
   454→    "validate_reflect_skip_evidence",
   455→    "validate_report_has_file_paths",
   456→    "validate_report_references_clusters",
   457→]
   458→
```

```
    32→class _DetectorNamesCacheCompat:
    33→    """Compat shim for tests that poke the legacy detector-name cache."""
    34→
    35→    def __init__(self) -> None:
    36→        self._store: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    37→
    38→    def __contains__(self, key: object) -> bool:
    39→        return key in self._store
    40→
    41→    def __getitem__(self, key: str) -> list[str]:
    42→        return self._store[key]
    43→
    44→    def __setitem__(self, key: str, value: list[str]) -> None:
    45→        self._store[key] = value
    46→
    47→    def pop(self, key: str, default: list[str] | None = None) -> list[str] | None:
    48→        return self._store.pop(key, default)
    49→
    50→
    51→_DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE = _DetectorNamesCacheCompat()
    52→
    53→
    54→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
    55→def _get_detector_names_cached() -> tuple[str, ...]:
    56→    """Compute detector names once until cache invalidation."""
    57→    return tuple(detector_names())
    58→
    59→
    60→def _get_detector_names() -> list[str]:
    61→    """Return cached detector names, computing on first access."""
    62→    return list(_get_detector_names_cached())
    63→
    64→
    65→def _invalidate_detector_names_cache() -> None:
    66→    """Invalidate detector-name cache when runtime registrations change."""
    67→    _get_detector_names_cached.cache_clear()
    68→    _DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE.pop("names", None)
    69→
    70→
    71→on_detector_registered(_invalidate_detector_names_cache)

    74→def create_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    75→    """Return the top-level argparse parser."""
    76→    return _create_parser(langs=available_langs(), detector_names=_get_detector_names())

    78→
    79→def _apply_persisted_exclusions(
    80→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    81→    config: Mapping[str, Any],
    82→) -> None:
    83→    """Merge CLI --exclude with persisted config.exclude and apply globally."""
    84→    cli_exclusions = getattr(args, "exclude", None) or []
    85→    persisted_raw = config.get("exclude", [])
    86→    persisted = (
    87→        [entry for entry in persisted_raw if isinstance(entry, str)]
    88→        if isinstance(persisted_raw, list)
    89→        else []
    90→    )
    91→    combined = list(cli_exclusions) + [e for e in persisted if e not in cli_exclusions]
    92→    if not combined:
    93→        return
    94→    set_exclusions(combined)
    95→    if cli_exclusions:
    96→        print(
    97→            colorize(f"  Excluding: {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"),
    98→            file=sys.stderr,
    99→        )
   100→        return
   101→    print(
   102→        colorize(
   103→            f"  Excluding (from config): {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"
   104→        ),
   105→        file=sys.stderr,
   106→    )
   107→
   108→
   109→def _project_root_from_state_path(state_path_value: str | Path | None) -> Path | None:
   110→    """Infer project root from an explicit state path under ``.desloppify``."""
   111→    if state_path_value in (None, ""):
   112→        return None
   113→    try:
   114→        state_file = Path(state_path_value).resolve()
   115→    except OSError:
   116→        return None
   117→    if state_file.parent.name != ".desloppify":
   118→        return None
   119→    if state_file.name == "state.json" or (
   120→        state_file.name.startswith("state-") and state_file.suffix == ".json"
   121→    ):
   122→        return state_file.parent.parent
   123→    return None
   124→
   125→
   126→def _resolve_default_path(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   127→    """Fill args.path from detected language or default source path.
   128→
   129→    For the review command, the last scan path (stored in state) is used as the
   130→    default so that ``desloppify review --prepare`` works on the same scope as
   131→    the preceding scan even when the project files are not under ``src/``.
   132→    """
   133→    if getattr(args, "path", None) is not None:
   134→        return
   135→    runtime_root = get_project_root()
   136→    if getattr(args, "command", None) == "review":
   137→        try:
   138→            state_file = state_path(args)
   139→            if state_file:
   140→                saved = load_state(state_file)
   141→                saved_path = saved.get("scan_path")
   142→                if saved_path:
   143→                    args.path = str((runtime_root / saved_path).resolve())
   144→                    return
   145→        except (OSError, KeyError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
   146→            log_best_effort_failure(logger, "resolve default review path from saved state", exc)
   147→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   148→    args.path = str(
   149→        get_default_scan_path(
   150→            project_root=runtime_root,
   151→            default_src=getattr(lang, "default_src", None),
   152→        )
   153→    )
   154→
   155→
   156→def _load_shared_runtime(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   157→    """Load config/state and attach shared objects to parsed args."""
   158→    config = load_config()
   159→
   160→    state_file = state_path(args)
   161→    state = load_state(state_file)
   162→    _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
   163→
   164→    args.runtime = CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)
   165→
   166→
   167→def _looks_like_desloppify_checkout(root: Path) -> bool:
   168→    """Return True when *root* appears to be the desloppify source checkout."""
   169→    package_dir = root / "desloppify"
   170→    pyproject = root / "pyproject.toml"
   171→    if not package_dir.is_dir() or not (package_dir / "__init__.py").is_file():
   172→        return False
   173→    if not pyproject.is_file():
   174→        return False
   175→    try:
   176→        text = pyproject.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   177→    except OSError:
   178→        return False
   179→    return 'name = "desloppify"' in text or "name='desloppify'" in text
   180→
   181→
   182→def _running_installed_package_from_checkout(
   183→    *,
   184→    cwd_root: Path | None = None,
   185→    module_file: str | Path | None = None,
   186→) -> bool:
   187→    """Detect running an installed package while standing in a repo checkout."""
   188→    root = (cwd_root or get_project_root()).resolve()
   189→    if not _looks_like_desloppify_checkout(root):
   190→        return False
   191→    current_module = Path(module_file or __file__).resolve()
   192→    try:
   193→        current_module.relative_to(root)
   194→        return False
   195→    except ValueError:
   196→        return True
   197→
   198→
   199→def _warn_if_running_installed_package_from_checkout() -> None:
   200→    """Emit a warning when CLI resolution bypasses the current checkout."""
   201→    if not _running_installed_package_from_checkout():
   202→        return
   203→    root = get_project_root().resolve()
   204→    print(
   205→        colorize(
   206→            "  WARNING: running installed desloppify package while current directory "
   207→            "looks like the desloppify checkout.",
   208→            "yellow",
   209→        ),
   210→        file=sys.stderr,
   211→    )
   212→    print(
   213→        colorize(
   214→            f"  Current checkout: {root}",
   215→            "dim",
   216→        ),
   217→        file=sys.stderr,
   218→    )
   219→    print(
   220→        colorize(
   221→            "  Use `python -m desloppify ...` or `./.venv/bin/desloppify ...` here "
   222→            "to run the local checkout instead of the installed package.",
   223→            "dim",
   224→        ),
   225→        file=sys.stderr,
   226→    )
   227→
   228→
   229→def _resolve_handler(command: str) -> CommandHandler:
   230→    """Resolve a CLI command name to its typed handler callable."""
   231→    return get_command_handlers()[command]
   232→
   233→
   234→def _handle_help_command(
   235→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   236→    parser: argparse.ArgumentParser,
   237→) -> None:
   238→    """Handle explicit help command when present in parser config."""
   239→    topic = list(getattr(args, "topic", []) or [])
   240→    try:
   241→        parser.parse_args([*topic, "--help"])
   242→    except SystemExit:
   243→        return
   244→
   245→
   246→def main() -> None:
   247→    # Ensure Unicode output works on Windows terminals (cp1252 etc.)
   248→    for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
   249→        if hasattr(stream, "reconfigure"):
   250→            try:
   251→                stream.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   252→            except (AttributeError, OSError):
   253→                logger.debug(
   254→                    "Skipping stream reconfigure for %s (not supported)",
   255→                    getattr(stream, "name", "<stream>"),
   256→                )
   257→
   258→    parser = create_parser()
   259→    args = parser.parse_args()
   260→    if not args.command:
   261→        parser.print_help()
   262→        return
   263→    if args.command == "help":
   264→        _handle_help_command(args, parser)
   265→        return
   266→
   267→    try:
   268→        with runtime_scope() as runtime:
   269→            inferred = _project_root_from_state_path(getattr(args, "state", None))
   270→            if inferred is not None:
   271→                runtime.project_root = inferred
   272→            _warn_if_running_installed_package_from_checkout()
   273→            _resolve_default_path(args)
   274→            _load_shared_runtime(args)
   275→
   276→            handler = _resolve_handler(args.command)
   277→            handler(args)
   278→    except CommandError as exc:
   279→        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   280→        sys.exit(exc.exit_code)
   281→    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   282→        print("\nInterrupted.")
   283→        sys.exit(1)
   284→
   285→
   286→if __name__ == "__main__":
   287→    main()
   288→
```

Excellent! Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a comprehensive summary of my findings:

## Summary of Findings

I've thoroughly searched the triage command code to verify the five claims from the GitHub issue. Here are my findings:

### 1. **Coverage Ledger Format Documentation** ✓ FOUND
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/stage_prompts.py` (lines 73-79)

The stage prompt documents the required Coverage Ledger format:
```python
"## Coverage Ledger Template\n"
"Your final report MUST contain a `## Coverage Ledger` section with one line per issue.\n"
"Allowed forms:\n"
'- `- abcd1234 -> cluster "cluster-name"`\n'
'- `- abcd1234 -> skip "specific-reason-tag"`\n'
```

**Arrow requirement confirmed:** The prompt explicitly shows the `->` arrow as required syntax. The parsing code in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/validation/core.py` lines 345-372 confirms this via `_extract_ledger_entry()` which returns `("", None, None)` if no `->` is present (line 372).

### 2. **Issue ID Validation in Observe Stage** ✓ CONFIRMED
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/validation/core.py` (lines 502-533)

The fallback validation code (when no Coverage Ledger is found) checks issue IDs using:
```python
for issue_id in valid_ids:
    if issue_id in report:  # Line 522
        cited.add(issue_id)
```

**The issue claim is CORRECT:** This uses full `issue_id in report` string matching, which means users must cite the full issue ID format (e.g., `review::.::holistic::12345678`). Short slugs or hashes alone won't work unless the Coverage Ledger section is present (which supports short hash resolution).

### 3. **Sense-Check Scope Validation** ✓ ISSUE CONFIRMED
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/sense_check.py` (lines 56-75)

The sense-check validation calls `evaluate_enrich_quality()`:
```python
quality_report = evaluate_enrich_quality(
    plan,
    repo_root,
    phase_label="sense-check",
    bad_paths_severity="failure",
    ...
)
```

**The issue claim appears CORRECT:** The validation runs `_steps_with_bad_paths(plan, repo_root)` which iterates through ALL clusters in the plan (see `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/validation/enrich_checks.py` lines 91-106). There is no scoping to only the current triage cycle's clusters - it validates paths across all clusters present in the plan.

### 4. **Bad File Path Error Messages** ✓ ISSUE CONFIRMED
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/stages/enrich.py` (lines 187-196)

The error message displays:
```python
f"  Warning: {bad_paths.total} file path(s) in step details don't exist on disk:"
for name, step_num, paths in bad_paths.rows[:5]:
    print(f"    {name} step {step_num}: {', '.join(paths[:3])}", "yellow")
```

**The issue claim is CORRECT:** The message shows a count of bad paths (`bad_paths.total`) and which cluster/step has them, but the actual implementation shows individual bad paths in each row (lines 195-196). However, the initial error message only shows the count, not the complete enumeration of which specific paths are bad. The detailed paths are shown per cluster/step, but only the first 3 per step and only the first 5 clusters.

### 5. **Report Input Argument** ✓ CONFIRMED
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_plan_impl_sections_triage_commit_scan.py` (lines 31-32)

The CLI parser defines:
```python
"--report", type=str, default=None,
help="Stage report text",
```

**The issue claim is CORRECT:** Only `--report` string argument is available. There is NO `--report-file` option for loading report text from a file - only inline string input via `--report`.

```
   100→def _effective_completion_strategy_summary(
   101→    *,
   102→    completion_mode: str,
   103→    strategy: str,
   104→    existing_strategy: str,
   105→    completion_note: str,
   106→) -> str:
   107→    """Return the strategy summary that should be stored after completion.
   108→
   109→    All text arguments should already be normalised via ``_normalize_summary_text``.
   110→    """
   111→    if strategy.lower() != "same":
   112→        return strategy
   113→
   114→    if completion_mode == "confirm_existing":
   115→        summary = (
   116→            "Reused the existing enriched cluster plan after re-review instead "
   117→            "of materializing a new reflect blueprint."
   118→        )
   119→        if completion_note:
   120→            summary += f" Reason: {completion_note}"
   121→        elif existing_strategy:
   122→            summary += " Prior execution sequencing remains in force."
   123→        return _truncate_summary_text(summary)
   124→
   125→    return existing_strategy
   126→

   128→def _normalized_issue_id_list(raw_ids: object) -> list[str]:
   129→    if not isinstance(raw_ids, list):
   130→        return []
   131→    return [issue_id for issue_id in raw_ids if isinstance(issue_id, str)]

   134→def live_active_triage_issue_ids(
   135→    plan: PlanModel,
   136→    state: StateModel | None = None,
   137→) -> set[str]:
   138→    """Return frozen triage IDs that are still open review issues in state."""
   139→    frozen_ids = active_triage_issue_ids(plan, state)
   140→    if state is None or not frozen_ids:
   141→        return frozen_ids
   142→    return frozen_ids & open_review_ids(state)

   145→def active_triage_issue_ids(
   146→    plan: PlanModel,
   147→    state: StateModel | None = None,
   148→) -> set[str]:
   149→    """Return the frozen review issue set for the current triage run.
   150→
   151→    Falls back to current open review IDs when no active set has been frozen yet.
   152→    """
   153→    meta = _ensure_triage_meta(plan)
   154→    active_ids = _normalized_issue_id_list(meta.get(_ACTIVE_TRIAGE_ISSUE_IDS_KEY))
   155→    if active_ids:
   156→        return set(active_ids)
   157→    if state is None:
   158→        return set()
   159→    return _coverage_open_ids(plan, state)
   160→
   161→
   162→def ensure_active_triage_issue_ids(
   163→    plan: PlanModel,
   164→    state: StateModel,
   165→) -> list[str]:
   166→    """Freeze the current triage issue set for validation across stage reruns."""
   167→    meta = _ensure_triage_meta(plan)
   168→    active_ids = sorted(_coverage_open_ids(plan, state))
   169→    meta[_ACTIVE_TRIAGE_ISSUE_IDS_KEY] = active_ids
   170→    meta.pop(_UNDISPOSITIONED_TRIAGE_ISSUES_KEY, None)
   171→    meta.pop(_UNDISPOSITIONED_TRIAGE_COUNT_KEY, None)
   172→    return active_ids

   175→def clear_active_triage_issue_tracking(meta: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   176→    """Clear frozen triage coverage metadata after successful completion."""
   177→    meta.pop(_ACTIVE_TRIAGE_ISSUE_IDS_KEY, None)
   178→    meta.pop(_UNDISPOSITIONED_TRIAGE_ISSUES_KEY, None)
   179→    meta.pop(_UNDISPOSITIONED_TRIAGE_COUNT_KEY, None)

   181→
   182→def undispositioned_triage_issue_ids(
   183→    plan: PlanModel,
   184→    state: StateModel | None = None,
   185→) -> list[str]:
   186→    """Return frozen triage issues still lacking cluster/skip/dismiss coverage."""
   187→    target_ids = live_active_triage_issue_ids(plan, state)
   188→    if not target_ids:
   189→        return []
   190→    covered_ids: set[str] = set()
   191→    for cluster in _ensure_cluster_map(plan).values():
   192→        if cluster.get("auto"):
   193→            continue
   194→        covered_ids.update(cluster_issue_ids(cluster))
   195→    covered_ids.update(
   196→        issue_id for issue_id in _ensure_skipped_map(plan) if isinstance(issue_id, str)
   197→    )
   198→    covered_ids.update(
   199→        _normalized_issue_id_list(_ensure_triage_meta(plan).get("dismissed_ids"))
   200→    )
   201→    return sorted(issue_id for issue_id in target_ids if issue_id not in covered_ids)
   202→
   203→
   204→def sync_undispositioned_triage_meta(
   205→    plan: PlanModel,
   206→    state: StateModel | None = None,
   207→) -> list[str]:
   208→    """Persist the current undispositioned triage issue set for recovery UX."""
   209→    meta = _ensure_triage_meta(plan)
   210→    missing = undispositioned_triage_issue_ids(plan, state)
   211→    if missing:
   212→        meta[_UNDISPOSITIONED_TRIAGE_ISSUES_KEY] = missing
   213→        meta[_UNDISPOSITIONED_TRIAGE_COUNT_KEY] = len(missing)
   214→    else:
   215→        meta.pop(_UNDISPOSITIONED_TRIAGE_ISSUES_KEY, None)
   216→        meta.pop(_UNDISPOSITIONED_TRIAGE_COUNT_KEY, None)
   217→    return missing

   231→def inject_triage_stages(plan: PlanModel) -> None:

   235→    order[:] = [*remaining, *TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS]

   244→def cascade_clear_later_confirmations(
   245→    stages: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],
   246→    from_stage: str,
   247→) -> list[str]:
   248→    try:
   249→        idx = _STAGE_ORDER.index(from_stage)
   250→    except ValueError:
   251→        return []
   252→    cleared: list[str] = []
   253→    for later in _STAGE_ORDER[idx + 1:]:
   254→        if later in stages and stages[later].get("confirmed_at"):
   255→            stages[later].pop("confirmed_at", None)
   256→            stages[later].pop("confirmed_text", None)
   257→            cleared.append(later)
   258→    return cleared
   259→
   260→
   261→def print_cascade_clear_feedback(
   262→    cleared: list[str],
   263→    stages: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],
   264→) -> None:
   265→    if not cleared:
   266→        return
   267→    print(colorize(f"  Cleared confirmations on: {', '.join(cleared)}", "yellow"))
   268→    next_unconfirmed = next(
   269→        (s for s in _STAGE_ORDER if s in stages and not stages[s].get("confirmed_at")),
   270→        None,
   271→    )
   272→    if next_unconfirmed:
   273→        print(colorize(
   274→            f"  Re-confirm with: desloppify plan triage --confirm {next_unconfirmed}",
   275→            "dim",
   276→        ))

   278→
   279→def observe_dimension_breakdown(si: TriageInput) -> tuple[dict[str, int], list[str]]:
   280→    by_dim: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
   281→    for _fid, issue in si.open_issues.items():
   282→        detail = issue.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(issue.get("detail"), dict) else {}
   283→        dim = detail.get("dimension", "unknown")
   284→        by_dim[dim] += 1
   285→    dim_names = sorted(by_dim, key=lambda d: (-by_dim[d], d))
   286→    return dict(by_dim), dim_names
   287→
   288→
   289→def group_issues_into_observe_batches(
   290→    si: TriageInput,
   291→    max_batches: int = 5,
   292→) -> list[tuple[list[str], dict[str, Issue]]]:
   293→    """Group observe issues into dimension-balanced batches."""
   294→    by_dim, dim_names = observe_dimension_breakdown(si)
   295→
   296→    if len(dim_names) <= 1:
   297→        return [(dim_names, dict(si.open_issues))]
   298→
   299→    num_batches = min(max_batches, len(dim_names))
   300→    batch_dims: list[list[str]] = [[] for _ in range(num_batches)]
   301→    batch_counts: list[int] = [0] * num_batches
   302→
   303→    for dim in dim_names:
   304→        lightest = min(range(num_batches), key=lambda i: batch_counts[i])
   305→        batch_dims[lightest].append(dim)
   306→        batch_counts[lightest] += by_dim[dim]
   307→
   308→    dim_to_issues: dict[str, dict[str, Issue]] = defaultdict(dict)
   309→    for fid, issue in si.open_issues.items():
   310→        detail = issue.get("detail", {}) if isinstance(issue.get("detail"), dict) else {}
   311→        dim = detail.get("dimension", "unknown")
   312→        dim_to_issues[dim][fid] = issue
   313→
   314→    result: list[tuple[list[str], dict[str, Issue]]] = []
   315→    for dims in batch_dims:
   316→        if not dims:
   317→            continue
   318→        subset: dict[str, Issue] = {}
   319→        for dim in dims:
   320→            subset.update(dim_to_issues.get(dim, {}))
   321→        if subset:
   322→            result.append((dims, subset))
   323→
   324→    return result
   325→

   327→def open_review_ids_from_state(state: StateModel) -> set[str]:
   328→    return open_review_ids(state)
   329→
   330→
   331→def has_open_review_issues(state: StateModel | dict | None) -> bool:
   332→    return bool(open_review_ids_from_state(state or {}))

   334→
   335→def cluster_issue_ids(cluster: Cluster) -> list[str]:
   336→    """Return the effective issue IDs for a cluster.
   337→
   338→    Falls back to ``action_steps[*].issue_refs`` when ``issue_ids`` is empty,
   339→    since some saved plans retain membership only in step refs.
   340→    """
   341→    ordered: list[str] = []
   342→    seen: set[str] = set()
   343→
   344→    def _append(raw_ids: object) -> None:
   345→        if not isinstance(raw_ids, list):
   346→            return
   347→        for raw_id in raw_ids:
   348→            if not isinstance(raw_id, str):
   349→                continue
   350→            issue_id = raw_id.strip()
   351→            if not issue_id or issue_id in seen:
   352→                continue
   353→            seen.add(issue_id)
   354→            ordered.append(issue_id)
   355→
   356→    _append(cluster.get("issue_ids"))
   357→
   358→    steps = cluster.get("action_steps")
   359→    if isinstance(steps, list):
   360→        for step in steps:
   361→            if not isinstance(step, dict):
   362→                continue
   363→            _append(step.get("issue_refs"))
   364→
   365→    return ordered
   366→
   367→
   368→
   369→def plan_review_ids(plan: PlanModel) -> list[str]:
   370→    """Return review/concerns IDs currently represented in queue_order."""
   371→    return [
   372→        fid for fid in _ensure_queue_order(plan)
   373→        if not fid.startswith("triage::")
   374→        and not fid.startswith("workflow::")
   375→        and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
   376→    ]
   377→
   378→
   379→def triage_coverage(
   380→    plan: PlanModel,
   381→    open_review_ids: set[str] | None = None,
   382→) -> tuple[int, int, dict[str, Cluster]]:
   383→    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for review issues in triage.
   384→
   385→    When *open_review_ids* is provided, use it as the full set of review
   386→    issues (from state) instead of falling back to queue_order.
   387→    """
   388→    clusters = _ensure_cluster_map(plan)
   389→    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
   390→    for c in clusters.values():
   391→        all_cluster_ids.update(cluster_issue_ids(c))
   392→    if open_review_ids is not None:
   393→        review_ids = list(open_review_ids)
   394→    else:
   395→        review_ids = plan_review_ids(plan)
   396→    organized = sum(1 for fid in review_ids if fid in all_cluster_ids)
   397→    return organized, len(review_ids), clusters
   398→
   399→
   400→def manual_clusters_with_issues(plan: PlanModel) -> list[str]:
   401→    return [
   402→        name for name, c in _ensure_cluster_map(plan).items()
   403→        if cluster_issue_ids(c) and not c.get("auto")
   404→    ]
   405→
   406→
   407→def _coverage_open_ids(
   408→    plan: PlanModel,
   409→    state: StateModel,
   410→) -> set[str]:
   411→    active_ids = _normalized_issue_id_list(
   412→        _ensure_triage_meta(plan).get(_ACTIVE_TRIAGE_ISSUE_IDS_KEY)
   413→    )
   414→    if active_ids:
   415→        return set(active_ids)
   416→    has_completed_scan = bool(state.get("last_scan"))
   417→    coverage_open_ids = open_review_ids_from_state(state)
   418→    if not has_completed_scan and not coverage_open_ids:
   419→        return set(plan_review_ids(plan))
   420→    return coverage_open_ids
   421→
   422→
   423→def _sync_completion_meta(
   424→    *,
   425→    plan: PlanModel,
   426→    state: StateModel,
   427→    strategy: str,
   428→    completion_mode: str,
   429→    completion_note: str,
   430→    coverage_open_ids: set[str],
   431→) -> tuple[dict[str, Any], str]:
   432→    meta = _ensure_triage_meta(plan)
   433→    if state.get("last_scan"):
   434→        meta["issue_snapshot_hash"] = review_issue_snapshot_hash(state)
   435→    elif not meta.get("issue_snapshot_hash"):
   436→        meta.pop("issue_snapshot_hash", None)
   437→
   438→    normalized_strategy = _normalize_summary_text(strategy)
   439→    existing_strategy = _normalize_summary_text(meta.get("strategy_summary", ""))
   440→    normalized_note = _normalize_summary_text(completion_note)
   441→    effective_strategy_summary = _effective_completion_strategy_summary(
   442→        completion_mode=completion_mode,
   443→        strategy=normalized_strategy,
   444→        existing_strategy=existing_strategy,
   445→        completion_note=normalized_note,
   446→    )
   447→    meta["triaged_ids"] = sorted(coverage_open_ids)
   448→    if effective_strategy_summary:
   449→        meta["strategy_summary"] = effective_strategy_summary
   450→    meta["trigger"] = "confirm_existing" if completion_mode == "confirm_existing" else "manual_triage"
   451→    meta["last_completion_mode"] = completion_mode
   452→    if normalized_note:
   453→        meta["last_completion_note"] = normalized_note
   454→    else:
   455→        meta.pop("last_completion_note", None)
   456→    meta["last_completed_at"] = utc_now()
   457→    return meta, effective_strategy_summary
   458→
   459→
   460→def _archive_and_clear_triage_stages(
   461→    meta: dict[str, Any],
   462→    *,
   463→    effective_strategy_summary: str,
   464→    existing_strategy: str,
   465→    completion_mode: str,
   466→    completion_note: str,
   467→) -> None:
   468→    stages = meta.get("triage_stages", {})
   469→    normalized_note = _normalize_summary_text(completion_note)
   470→    if stages:
   471→        last_triage = {
   472→            "completed_at": utc_now(),
   473→            "stages": {k: dict(v) for k, v in stages.items()},
   474→            "strategy": effective_strategy_summary,
   475→            "completion_mode": completion_mode,
   476→        }
   477→        if completion_mode == "confirm_existing":
   478→            last_triage["reused_existing_plan"] = True
   479→            if normalized_note:
   480→                last_triage["completion_note"] = normalized_note
   481→            if existing_strategy and existing_strategy != effective_strategy_summary:
   482→                last_triage["previous_strategy_summary"] = existing_strategy
   483→        meta["last_triage"] = last_triage
   484→    meta["triage_stages"] = {}
   485→    meta.pop("triage_recommended", None)
   486→    meta.pop("stage_refresh_required", None)
   487→    meta.pop("stage_snapshot_hash", None)
   488→    clear_active_triage_issue_tracking(meta)
   489→
   490→
   491→def _print_completion_summary(
   492→    *,
   493→    plan: PlanModel,
   494→    clusters: dict[str, Cluster],
   495→    organized: int,
   496→    total: int,
   497→    completion_mode: str,
   498→    effective_strategy_summary: str,
   499→) -> None:
   500→    cluster_count = len([c for c in clusters.values() if cluster_issue_ids(c)])
   501→    print(colorize(f"  Triage complete: {organized}/{total} issues in {cluster_count} cluster(s).", "green"))
   502→    if completion_mode == "confirm_existing":
   503→        print(
   504→            colorize(
   505→                "  Completion mode: reused the current enriched cluster plan; "
   506→                "did not materialize a new reflect blueprint.",
   507→                "cyan",
   508→            )
   509→        )
   510→    if effective_strategy_summary:
   511→        print(colorize(f"  Strategy: {effective_strategy_summary}", "cyan"))
   512→    print(colorize("  Run `desloppify next` to start implementation.", "green"))
   513→
   514→def apply_completion(
   515→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   516→    plan: PlanModel,
   517→    strategy: str,
   518→    *,
   519→    services: TriageServices | None = None,
   520→    completion_mode: str = "manual_triage",
   521→    completion_note: str = "",
   522→) -> None:
   523→    """Shared completion logic: update meta, remove triage stage IDs, save."""
   524→    resolved_services = services or default_triage_services()
   525→    runtime = resolved_services.command_runtime(args)
   526→    state = runtime.state
   527→
   528→    coverage_open_ids = _coverage_open_ids(plan, state)
   529→    organized, total, clusters = triage_coverage(
   530→        plan, open_review_ids=coverage_open_ids,
   531→    )
   532→
   533→    purge_ids(plan, [
   534→        *TRIAGE_IDS,
   535→        WORKFLOW_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_ID,
   536→        WORKFLOW_CREATE_PLAN_ID,
   537→    ])
   538→
   539→    existing_strategy = _normalize_summary_text(
   540→        _ensure_triage_meta(plan).get("strategy_summary", ""),
   541→    )
   542→    meta, effective_strategy_summary = _sync_completion_meta(
   543→        plan=plan,
   544→        state=state,
   545→        strategy=strategy,
   546→        completion_mode=completion_mode,
   547→        completion_note=completion_note,
   548→        coverage_open_ids=coverage_open_ids,
   549→    )
   550→    _archive_and_clear_triage_stages(
   551→        meta,
   552→        effective_strategy_summary=effective_strategy_summary,
   553→        existing_strategy=existing_strategy,
   554→        completion_mode=completion_mode,
   555→        completion_note=completion_note,
   556→    )
   557→    resolved_services.save_plan(plan)
   558→    _print_completion_summary(
   559→        plan=plan,
   560→        clusters=clusters,
   561→        organized=organized,
   562→        total=total,
   563→        completion_mode=completion_mode,
   564→        effective_strategy_summary=effective_strategy_summary,
   565→    )
   566→
   567→
   568→def find_cluster_for(fid: str, clusters: dict[str, Cluster]) -> str | None:
   569→    for name, c in clusters.items():
   570→        if fid in cluster_issue_ids(c):
   571→            return name
   572→    return None
   573→
   574→
   575→def count_log_activity_since(plan: PlanModel, since: str) -> dict[str, int]:
   576→    counts: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
   577→    for raw_entry in _ensure_execution_log(plan):
   578→        if "timestamp" not in raw_entry or "action" not in raw_entry:
   579→            continue
   580→        timestamp = raw_entry["timestamp"]
   581→        action = raw_entry["action"]
   582→        if not isinstance(timestamp, str) or not isinstance(action, str):
   583→            continue
   584→        if timestamp >= since:
   585→            counts[action] += 1
   586→    return dict(counts)
   587→
   588→__all__ = [
   589→    "active_triage_issue_ids",
   590→    "apply_completion",
   591→    "cascade_clear_later_confirmations",
   592→    "clear_active_triage_issue_tracking",
   593→    "cluster_issue_ids",
   594→    "count_log_activity_since",
   595→    "ensure_active_triage_issue_ids",
   596→    "find_cluster_for",
   597→    "group_issues_into_observe_batches",
   598→    "has_open_review_issues",
   599→    "has_triage_in_queue",
   600→    "inject_triage_stages",
   601→    "live_active_triage_issue_ids",
```

```
1→"""Stage-gate and coverage helpers for triage command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     6→from desloppify.engine.plan_triage import TRIAGE_IDS
     7→
     8→from ..helpers import cluster_issue_ids, live_active_triage_issue_ids
     9→
    10→
    11→def _require_triage_pending(plan: dict, *, action: str) -> bool:
    12→    """Require at least one triage stage ID to be present in queue for an action."""
    13→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    14→    if order & TRIAGE_IDS:
    15→        return True
    16→    print(colorize(f"  No triage stage in the queue — nothing to {action}.", "yellow"))
    17→    return False
    18→
    19→
    20→def _validate_stage_report(
    21→    report: str | None,
    22→    *,
    23→    stage: str,
    24→    min_chars: int,
    25→    missing_guidance: list[str] | None = None,
    26→    short_guidance: list[str] | None = None,
    27→) -> str | None:
    28→    """Validate staged report presence/length and print consistent guidance."""
    29→    if not report:
    30→        print(colorize(f"  --report is required for --stage {stage}.", "red"))
    31→        for line in missing_guidance or []:
    32→            print(colorize(f"  {line}", "dim"))
    33→        return None
    34→    cleaned = report.strip()
    35→    if len(cleaned) < min_chars:
    36→        print(
    37→            colorize(
    38→                f"  Report too short: {len(cleaned)} chars (minimum {min_chars}).",
    39→                "red",
    40→            )
    41→        )
    42→        for line in short_guidance or []:
    43→            print(colorize(f"  {line}", "dim"))
    44→        return None
    45→    return cleaned
    46→
    47→
    48→def unenriched_clusters(plan: dict) -> list[tuple[str, list[str]]]:
    49→    """Return clusters with issues that are missing required enrichment.
    50→
    51→    Requirements:
    52→    - Every cluster needs a description and at least one action_step.
    53→    - Small clusters (< 5 issues) need at least 1 action step per issue,
    54→      so each item has a concrete plan. Large clusters (>= 5) just need
    55→      steps overall (cluster-level plan is sufficient).
    56→    """
    57→    gaps: list[tuple[str, list[str]]] = []
    58→    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
    59→        issue_ids = cluster_issue_ids(cluster)
    60→        if not issue_ids:
    61→            continue
    62→        if cluster.get("auto"):
    63→            continue
    64→        missing: list[str] = []
    65→        if not cluster.get("description"):
    66→            missing.append("description")
    67→        steps = cluster.get("action_steps") or []
    68→        issue_count = len(issue_ids)
    69→        if not steps:
    70→            missing.append("action_steps")
    71→        elif issue_count < 5 and len(steps) < issue_count:
    72→            missing.append(
    73→                f"action_steps (have {len(steps)}, need >= {issue_count} for small cluster)"
    74→            )
    75→        if missing:
    76→            gaps.append((name, missing))
    77→    return gaps
    78→
    79→
    80→def unclustered_review_issues(plan: dict, state: dict | None = None) -> list[str]:
    81→    """Return review issue IDs that aren't in any manual cluster.
    82→
    83→    When *state* is provided, uses open review/concerns issues from state
    84→    (the canonical source). Falls back to scanning queue_order for backwards
    85→    compatibility.
    86→    """
    87→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    88→    clustered_ids: set[str] = set()
    89→    for cluster in clusters.values():
    90→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
    91→            clustered_ids.update(cluster_issue_ids(cluster))
    92→    skipped_ids = {
    93→        fid for fid in (plan.get("skipped", {}) or {}).keys() if isinstance(fid, str)
    94→    }
    95→
    96→    if state is not None:
    97→        review_ids = [
    98→            fid for fid, finding in state.get("issues", {}).items()
    99→            if finding.get("status") == "open"
   100→            and finding.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
   101→        ]
   102→        frozen_ids = (plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {}) or {}).get("active_triage_issue_ids")
   103→        if isinstance(frozen_ids, list) and frozen_ids:
   104→            frozen_id_set = live_active_triage_issue_ids(plan, state)
   105→            review_ids = [fid for fid in review_ids if fid in frozen_id_set]
   106→    else:
   107→        review_ids = [
   108→            fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
   109→            if not fid.startswith("triage::") and not fid.startswith("workflow::")
   110→            and (fid.startswith("review::") or fid.startswith("concerns::"))
   111→        ]
   112→
   113→    return [
   114→        fid for fid in review_ids
   115→        if fid not in clustered_ids and fid not in skipped_ids
   116→    ]
   117→
```

```
    10→def resolve_reusable_report(
    11→    report: str | None,
    12→    existing_stage: dict | None,
    13→) -> tuple[str | None, bool]:
    14→    if report:

    16→    if existing_stage and existing_stage.get("report"):
    17→        return existing_stage["report"], True
    18→    return None, False

    21→def record_observe_stage(
    22→    stages: dict,
    23→    *,

    25→    issue_count: int,
    26→    cited_ids: list[str],
    27→    existing_stage: dict | None,

    43→    cleared = cascade_clear_later_confirmations(stages, "observe")
```

```
title:	Enforce executor-ready detail standard for all queue items (gold standard: payments-auth-hardening)
state:	OPEN
author:	peteromallet
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	397
--
## Problem

The desloppify queue currently produces clusters with wildly varying quality levels. Roughly 60% of items have action steps that are too vague to execute without significant investigation by the developer — defeating the purpose of a pre-analyzed queue.

## The gold standard: `payments-auth-hardening`

This cluster demonstrates what every queue item should look like. Its steps are **mechanically executable** — a developer who has never seen the codebase could complete them without guessing:

- **Step 1:** "In `supabase/functions/setup-auto-topup/index.ts` at lines 15-20, 43-49, and 74-90, remove `stripeCustomerId` and `stripePaymentMethodId` from the documented request body, body destructuring, and users update payload."
- **Step 2:** References `session.metadata.userId` at specific lines with exact changes.
- **Step 3:** Provides specific migration SQL patterns to apply.

Key properties:
- **Exact file paths with line numbers**
- **Specific field/function names** to change
- **Exact changes** to make (remove X, add Y, rename Z)
- A developer who has never seen the codebase could execute them mechanically

## The problem cases (~60% of items)

Many items use vague language that requires the executor to re-do the analysis the tool should have already done:

- "Consider splitting into 2-3 composed hooks" — which hooks? Split into what?
- "Gradually move domain-specific shared code" — which code? Which domains? To where?
- "Refactor", "clean up", "improve" without file paths and line numbers
- "Add tests for X" without specifying what behavior to test

These aren't actionable queue items — they're observations dressed up as tasks.

## Requested changes

### 1. Stricter enrich-stage validation

The enrich stage should reject or re-process steps that:
- Use vague verbs like "consider", "gradually", "improve", "refactor", "clean up" without accompanying file paths and line numbers
- Reference files without line numbers or specific identifiers (function names, variable names, etc.)
- Describe a direction without specifying the destination ("move X somewhere better")

Validation heuristic: if a step doesn't contain at least one file path and one specific identifier (line number, function name, field name), it likely isn't executor-ready.

### 2. Test coverage items should include behavioral specs

Instead of:
> "Add test coverage for `useAutoTopup` hook"

Require:
> "In `src/hooks/__tests__/useAutoTopup.test.ts`, add tests for: (1) successful topup triggers payment method validation, (2) insufficient balance returns specific error, (3) concurrent calls are debounced"

The item should specify **what to test** (behaviors, edge cases), not just **which file** to add tests to.

### 3. Flag architecture observations separately from executable tasks

Some items are legitimate observations but aren't queue-executable:
- "This module has grown to 500 lines and mixes concerns"
- "The data flow here is circular and should be rethought"

These should be flagged as a different category (e.g., `observation` or `design-note`) rather than appearing alongside mechanically executable tasks. They pollute the queue and inflate item counts without being completable in the same way.

### 4. Use `payments-auth-hardening` as the reference standard

Consider using this cluster (or a templatized version of it) as the internal benchmark during enrichment. After enriching a cluster, the tool could self-evaluate: "Would a developer who has never seen this codebase be able to execute every step without opening any files first?" If not, the steps need more detail.

## Summary

The tool already demonstrates it *can* produce gold-standard output — `payments-auth-hardening` proves that. The request is to make that level of detail the enforced minimum, not the exception.
```

```
title:	Triage process has significant friction for AI agents (and probably humans too)
state:	OPEN
author:	peteromallet
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	398
--
## Context

Just completed a full 5-stage triage cycle (observe → reflect → organize → enrich → sense-check) for 42 review issues across 17 dimensions. The process took ~45 minutes of wall time with heavy parallelization. Filing this to capture the friction points while they're fresh.

## Friction Points (ordered by time wasted)

### 1. Coverage Ledger format undocumented — cost: ~15 minutes of retries

The reflect stage requires a `## Coverage Ledger` section, but the expected **line format** is not documented in the stage prompt. The validator uses `_extract_ledger_entry()` which requires `->` arrows:
```
- issue_slug -> cluster "cluster-name"
```

I tried these formats first (all rejected silently with "0 issue IDs cited"):
- `- issue_slug [hash]` (YAML-style)
- `hash: issue_slug` (structured assessment style)
- `issue_slug, issue_slug, issue_slug` (comma-separated per cluster)
- Full issue IDs as substrings

**Suggestion**: Add the expected format to the stage prompt. Something like:
```
Each line in the Coverage Ledger must follow: `- <issue_slug> -> <decision> "<target>"`
Example: `- auto_topup_trusts_client -> cluster "payments-auth-hardening"`
```

### 2. Issue ID citation format ambiguous — cost: ~10 minutes

The observe stage says "cite specific issue IDs (hash prefixes like [abcd1234])". But:
- Content hashes (8-char hex like `7d3ad98e`) weren't recognized
- Short slugs (`auto_topup_trusts_client_supplied_payment_ids`) weren't recognized  
- Only full issue IDs (`review::.::holistic::authorization_consistency::auto_topup_trusts_client_supplied_payment_ids`) as literal substrings worked

The validator uses `if issue_id in report` where `issue_id` is the full `review::.::holistic::...` string. The fallback hex matching also didn't work in practice.

**Suggestion**: Either make the validator accept short slugs/hashes, or document that full IDs are required.

### 3. Sense-check blocks on ALL cluster paths, not just current triage — cost: blocking

The sense-check stage validates file paths across ALL clusters in the plan, including old clusters from previous triage cycles. When the codebase evolves (files renamed/moved), these old clusters accumulate bad paths. I found 100+ bad paths across ~20 clusters from previous cycles.

This means completing a new triage cycle requires fixing paths in clusters you didn't touch and that may not even be in the current queue.

**Suggestion**: Sense-check should only validate clusters that are part of the current triage run, or at minimum provide `--skip-stale-clusters` to scope the check.

### 4. Bad file path error messages are non-specific

When validation says "1 file path(s) don't exist on disk", it doesn't say WHICH path or which cluster/step. You have to write custom Python to scan the plan JSON and check every extracted path against disk.

**Suggestion**: Print the bad paths in the error message, like: `Bad path in error-contract-normalization step 2: src/foo/bar.ts`

### 5. Five mandatory stages is heavy when clusters already exist

This triage cycle was enriching *existing* clusters that were already well-organized from a previous cycle. The observe → reflect → organize stages were largely rubber-stamping existing work. The real value was in the enrich stage.

**Suggestion**: Allow `--skip-to-enrich` when clusters already exist and are populated. Or make stages skippable with attestation.

### 6. Shell escaping on long reports

Reports with 42 issue IDs easily hit 2000+ characters. Passing them via `--report "..."` through shell quoting is fragile — underscores near quotes, newlines in HEREDOC, etc. Using `subprocess.run()` from Python was more reliable than any shell approach.

**Suggestion**: Support `--report-file path/to/report.txt` to read from a file.

## What Worked Well

- The **enrich stage prompt** is excellent — the gold standard example, the "lazy patterns to avoid" section, and the path validation are all genuinely useful.
- **Cluster management CLI** (`cluster show`, `cluster update --update-step`) is well-designed.
- **Subagent parallelization** guidance in the stage prompts is good.
- The **payments-auth-hardening cluster** level of detail is what every queue item should aspire to.

## Environment
- desloppify 0.9.5
- Claude Code (Opus 4.6) as the agent
- 42 review issues, 17 dimensions, 13 clusters

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
```

```
   122→
   123→def _missing_stage_prerequisite(
   124→    stages: dict,
   125→    *,
   126→    flow: str,
   127→) -> StagePrerequisite | None:
   128→    """Return the first missing upstream stage required by one triage flow."""
   129→    for prerequisite in _STAGE_PREREQUISITES.get(flow, ()):
   130→        stage_record = stages.get(prerequisite.stage_name)
   131→        if stage_record is None:
   132→            return prerequisite
   133→        if prerequisite.require_confirmation and not stage_record.get("confirmed_at"):
   134→            return prerequisite
   135→    return None
   136→
   137→
   138→def require_stage_prerequisite(
   139→    stages: dict,
   140→    *,
   141→    flow: str,
   142→    messages: dict[str, tuple[str, str]],
   143→) -> bool:
   144→    """Print consistent prerequisite guidance for simple triage stage gates."""
   145→    missing = _missing_stage_prerequisite(stages, flow=flow)
   146→    if missing is None:
   147→        return True
   148→    blocked_heading, command_hint = messages[missing.stage_name]
   149→    print(colorize(blocked_heading, "red"))
   150→    print(colorize(command_hint, "dim"))
   151→    return False
   152→
   153→
   154→def _auto_confirm_stage(
   155→    *,
   156→    plan: dict,
   157→    stage_record: dict,
   158→    attestation: str | None,
   159→    request: AutoConfirmStageRequest,
   160→    save_plan_fn=None,
   161→) -> bool:
   162→    """Shared auto-confirm flow for stage fold-confirm operations."""
   163→    if save_plan_fn is None:
   164→        save_plan_fn = save_plan
   165→    if stage_record.get("confirmed_at"):
   166→        return True
   167→    if not attestation or len(attestation.strip()) < MIN_ATTESTATION_LEN:
   168→        print(colorize(f"  {request.blocked_heading}", "red"))
   169→        print(colorize(f"  Run: {request.confirm_cmd}", "dim"))
   170→        print(colorize(f"  {request.inline_hint}", "dim"))
   171→        return False
   172→
   173→    confirmed_text = attestation.strip()
   174→    validation_err = validate_attestation(
   175→        confirmed_text,
   176→        request.stage_name,
   177→        dimensions=request.dimensions,
   178→        cluster_names=request.cluster_names,
   179→    )
   180→    if validation_err:
   181→        print(colorize(f"  {validation_err}", "red"))
   182→        return False
   183→
   184→    stage_record["confirmed_at"] = utc_now()
   185→    stage_record["confirmed_text"] = confirmed_text
   186→    save_plan_fn(plan)
   187→    print(colorize(f"  ✓ {request.stage_label} auto-confirmed via --attestation.", "green"))
   188→    return True

   219→def _validate_recurring_dimension_mentions(
   220→    *,
   221→    report: str,
   222→    recurring_dims: list[str],
   223→    recurring: dict,
   224→) -> bool:
   225→    if not recurring_dims:
   226→        return True
   227→    report_lower = report.lower()
   228→    mentioned = [dim for dim in recurring_dims if dim.lower() in report_lower]
   229→    if mentioned:
   230→        return True
   231→    print(colorize("  Recurring patterns detected but not addressed in report:", "red"))
   232→    for dim in recurring_dims:
   233→        info = recurring[dim]
   234→        print(
   235→            colorize(
   236→                f"    {dim}: {len(info['resolved'])} resolved, "
   237→                f"{len(info['open'])} still open — potential loop",
   238→                "yellow",
   239→            )
   240→        )
   241→    print(colorize("  Your report must mention at least one recurring dimension name.", "dim"))
   242→    return False
```
